category=ALFWarning)\n\n# --- constants ---\n\nHERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\n# Directory holding the one_cache/ folder. Harbor mounts the dataset at /app/data while\n# this script runs from /solution, so solve.sh overrides this with --datadir.\nDATA_DIR = os.path.join(HERE, 'data')\nRELEASE = 'Brainwidemap'\nONE_CACHE_DIRNAME = 'one_cache'\nBASE_URL = 'https://openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org'\nPASSWORD = 'international'\n# ONE expires a cached REST response five minutes after it is written. The cache shipped\n# with the dataset is pre-dated so it survives; this keeps what this run caches alive too.\nREST_EXPIRY = datetime.timedelta(days=365 * 50)\n\nT_START, T_STOP = -0.5, 1.5      # decoding window around stimulus onset, following the reference code\nBIN = 0.02\nN_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))            # 100\nEDGES = T_START + BIN * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\nTIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                              # bin centres, the first decoder input\n\n# reaction time bounds of the paper, which truncates the distribution at 80 ms and 2 s;\nMIN_RT, MAX_RT = 0.08, 2.0\n\nQC_LABEL = 1.0                   # the \"stringent quality control\" of the paper, 75,708 units of 621,733\nSPLIT = [100 / 3, 2 * 100 / 3]       # equal sized classes, from percentiles of each session's own trace\n\nCHOICE = {1.0: 0, -1.0: 1}       # +1 is a leftward choice, -1 rightward; 0 is a no response, dropped\nPRIOR = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\nMOVEMENT = ['low', 'medium', 'high']\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_stimulus_onset', 'trial_number_in_block']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['choice', 'prior_probability_left', 'wheel_speed', 'whisker_motion_energy']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [['left', 'right'], ['0.2', '0.5', '0.8'], MOVEMENT, MOVEMENT]\n\n# the cache location is not known until --datadir is parsed, and the connection cannot be\n# shared across a fork anyway, so connect() fills this in per process rather than at import\none = None\nsubset_csv = None    # DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv path, or None on the full dataset; set by connect()\nbrain_regions = BrainRegions()\n\n\ndef connect(datadir):\n    \"\"\"Point the module-level ONE client at the cache under datadir.\n\n    An Alyx-backed client is required: eid2pid, which load_neural() uses to find the probe\n    insertions, exists only on OneAlyx. mode='local' returns a plain One without it. The\n    client also resolves each dataset's current revision, which is what is on disk -- the\n    release tables list the pre-revision paths, so resolving from them alone finds no\n    trials. The metadata that resolution needs is cached under one_cache/.rest, so a run\n    does not have to reach Alyx.\n\n    datadir: directory holding the one_cache/ folder.\n    Sets the globals `one` and `subset_csv`; returns nothing.\n    \"\"\"\n    global one\n    global subset_csv\n    cache = os.path.join(datadir, ONE_CACHE_DIRNAME)\n    tables = os.path.join(cache, RELEASE)\n    client = dict(base_url=BASE_URL, silent=True, cache_dir=cache, tables_dir=tables,\n                  mode='remote')\n    try:\n        # No password: passing one forces re-authentication over the network, while the\n        # token the fetch cached needs no connection.\n        one = ONE(**client)\n    except Exception:\n        one = ONE(password=PASSWORD, **client)\n    one.alyx.default_expiry = REST_EXPIRY\n    # One.load_cache reads the parquet tables where they are; the OneAlyx override would\n    # ask Alyx for a newer copy first. In 'remote' mode nothing is loaded until this is\n    # called, and search() then returns no sessions at all, without raising.\n    One.load_cache(one, tables_dir=tables)\n    # Only the datalimit dataset ships this file; on the full dataset there is nothing to\n    # restrict to, and subset_csv stays None.\n    path = os.path.join(datadir, 'DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv')\n    subset_csv = path if os.path.isfile(path) else None\n\n\n# --- sessions ---\n\ndef select_sessions():\n    \"\"\"Released sessions that have everything the decoder needs, grouped by subject.\"\"\"\n\n    required = ['spikes.times.npy', 'spikes.clusters.npy',          # ephys\n                '_ibl_trials.table.pqt',                            # task\n                '_ibl_wheel.position.npy', '_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy']\n\n    keep = set(one.search(query_type='local', datasets=required))\n    keep &= (set(one.search(query_type='local', datasets=['leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n                                                          '_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy']))\n             | set(one.search(query_type='local', datasets=['rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n                                                            '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy'])))\n    # The datalimit dataset ships a subset of the release (see DATALIMIT_SUBSET.csv). The ONE\n    # release index is unmodified and still lists every released session, so\n    # restrict to the eids named there -- otherwise a session that is not on disk\n    # would be fetched over the network. The full dataset has no such file, and every\n    # session the index lists is on disk.\n    # Compare in string space: one.search returns UUID objects while the csv column\n    # is str, so a plain set intersection is empty whatever the contents. keep must\n    # stay UUID-valued for the `eid in keep` test below.\n    if subset_csv is not None:\n        subset_eids = set(pd.read_csv(subset_csv).eid.astype(str))\n        keep = {eid for eid in keep if str(eid) in subset_eids}\n        if not keep:\n            raise SystemExit(f'No sessions matched {subset_csv}; refusing to continue.')\n\n    eids, info = one.search(query_type='local', details=True)\n    selected = {}\n    for eid, session in zip(eids, info):\n        if eid in keep:\n            selected.setdefault(session['subject'], []).append(str(eid))\n    return {subject: selected[subject] for subject in sorted(selected)}\n\n\ndef camera_view(eid):\n    \"\"\"The side camera this session has whisker motion energy for, left preferred.\"\"\"\n    for view in ('left', 'right'):\n        if len(one.list_datasets(eid, filename=f'{view}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n                                 query_type='local')):\n            return view\n    raise ValueError(f'no whisker motion energy for {eid}')\n\n\n# --- trials ---\n\ndef trial_variables(trials):\n    \"\"\"Choice, block prior and trial number in block, one row per trial.\"\"\"\n    # probabilityLeft is constant within a block, so a change of value starts a new block\n    block = (trials.probabilityLeft != trials.probabilityLeft.shift()).cumsum()\n\n    return pd.DataFrame({'choice': trials.choice.map(CHOICE),\n                         'prior': trials.probabilityLeft.map(PRIOR),\n                         'trial_in_block': trials.groupby(block).cumcount()})\n\n\ndef covered(times, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Trials whose window is spanned by this stream, allowing the one bin gap the reference allows.\"\"\"\n    start, stop = stim_on + T_START, stim_on + T_STOP\n    begin = np.searchsorted(times, start, side='right')      # first sample inside the window\n    end = np.searchsorted(times, stop, side='left') - 1       # last sample inside the window\n\n    # a trial with no sample at all, or a gap at either edge, has no usable trace\n    inside = begin <= end\n    first = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(begin, 0, times.size - 1)], np.inf)\n    last = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(end, 0, times.size - 1)], -np.inf)\n    return inside & (np.abs(start - first) <= BIN) & (np.abs(stop - last) <= BIN)\n\n\ndef usable_trials(trials, wheel_times, camera_times):\n    \"\"\"Trials the paper would keep, that also have a full wheel and camera window.\"\"\"\n    stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()\n    reaction = trials.firstMovement_times.to_numpy() - stim_on\n\n    keep = (reaction >= MIN_RT) & (reaction <= MAX_RT)          # NaN compares false, so those go too\n    keep &= trials.choice.isin(CHOICE).to_numpy()               # drops the no response trials\n    keep &= trials.probabilityLeft.isin(PRIOR).to_numpy()\n\n    return keep & covered(wheel_times, stim_on) & covered(camera_times, stim_on)\n\n\n# --- behavior ---\n\ndef window_slice(times, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Index range of each trial window, so a stream is sliced rather than rescanned per trial.\"\"\"\n    window_start = stim_on + T_START\n    window_stop = stim_on + T_STOP\n\n    # the samples are in time order, so each window is one contiguous range of them\n    begin = np.searchsorted(times, window_start)\n    end = np.searchsorted(times, window_stop)\n\n    return begin, end\n\n\ndef trial_traces(times, values, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"A continuous trace resampled onto the same time grid for every trial.\"\"\"\n    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n\n    traces = []\n    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n        # one sample either side of the window, so the first and last bin centre are\n        # interpolated rather than clamped to the nearest sample inside it\n        first = max(begin[trial] - 1, 0)\n        last = end[trial] + 1\n\n        traces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n\n    return np.array(traces)\n\n\ndef discretize(trace):\n    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n\n\n# --- neural ---\n\ndef load_neural(eid):\n    \"\"\"Spike times and good clusters of a session, merged over its probes.\"\"\"\n    times, units, regions = [], [], []\n    for pid, name in zip(*one.eid2pid(eid)):\n        loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=name)\n\n        # skip the md5 check, which rereads every spike file and draws its own progress bar\n        spikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting(check_hash=False)\n\n        # a session can hold an insertion whose spike sorting was never released\n        if not clusters:\n            continue\n\n        clusters = loader.merge_clusters(spikes, clusters, channels)   # attaches metrics and histology\n\n        # `void` is the atlas label for a channel the histology placed outside the brain,\n        # so a unit there is not in a brain region at all\n        in_brain = brain_regions.acronym2acronym(\n            clusters['acronym'], mapping='Beryl') != 'void'\n\n        # spikes.clusters holds the row of the cluster table, so a per cluster flag\n        # can be indexed by it directly to say whether each spike survives\n        is_good = (clusters['label'] >= QC_LABEL) & in_brain\n        keep = is_good[spikes['clusters']]\n\n        # renumber the surviving clusters, continuing the count from the previous probe\n        new_index = np.cumsum(is_good) - 1\n        units.append(new_index[spikes['clusters'][keep]] + len(regions))\n        times.append(spikes['times'][keep])\n\n        # Beryl is the coarser atlas the reference code uses, e.g. DG-sg and DG-mo both become DG\n        regions.extend(brain_regions.acronym2acronym(clusters['acronym'][is_good], mapping='Beryl'))\n\n    times, units = np.concatenate(times), np.concatenate(units)\n\n    # sort the merged probes by spike time, as the reference does, so trials can be sliced\n    order = np.argsort(times, kind='stable')\n    return times[order], units[order], np.array(regions)\n\n\ndef spike_counts(times, units, n_units, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Spike count per unit and bin, for every trial.\"\"\"\n    counts = np.empty((stim_on.size, n_units, N_BINS))\n    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n\n    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n        spike_time = times[begin[trial]:end[trial]] - onset      # time from stimulus onset\n        spike_unit = units[begin[trial]:end[trial]]\n\n        bin_index = np.floor((spike_time - T_START) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n        \n        # a spike sitting on the window edge can round out of range once the onset is subtracted\n        bin_index = np.clip(bin_index, 0, N_BINS - 1)\n\n        # one flat index per spike lets a single bincount fill the whole unit by bin grid\n        flat_index = spike_unit * N_BINS + bin_index\n        counts[trial] = np.bincount(flat_index, minlength=n_units * N_BINS).reshape(n_units, N_BINS)\n\n    return counts\n\n\n# --- session ---\n\ndef process_session(eid, subject):\n    \"\"\"One session as lists of per trial neural, input and output arrays.\"\"\"\n    loader = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\n    loader.load_trials()\n    loader.load_wheel()\n    view = camera_view(eid)\n    loader.load_motion_energy(views=[view])\n\n    trials, wheel = loader.trials, loader.wheel\n    motion_energy = loader.motion_energy[f'{view}Camera']\n\n    variables = trial_variables(trials)                 # before filtering, so the block count is real\n    keep = usable_trials(trials, wheel.times.to_numpy(), motion_energy.times.to_numpy())\n    variables = variables[keep]\n    stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()[keep]\n\n    # no trial survived the mask, so there is nothing to load or bin\n    if stim_on.size == 0:\n        return {'session_id': str(eid), 'subject': subject, 'camera': view,\n                'neural': [], 'input': [], 'output': [],\n                'n_trials': 0, 'n_units': 0, 'regions': np.array([])}\n\n    speed = trial_traces(wheel.times, np.abs(wheel.velocity), stim_on)\n    whisker = trial_traces(motion_energy.times, motion_energy.iloc[:, 1], stim_on)\n\n    spike_times, spike_units, regions = load_neural(eid)\n    rate = spike_counts(spike_times, spike_units, regions.size, stim_on) / BIN\n\n    # Prior is stimulus-aligned but its paper-specific window is -0.6 to -0.1 s. Dynamic wheel/whisker analyses in the prose align to first movement and use 0 to +1 s. The supplied caching code instead uses the task-required common stimulus alignment of -0.5 to +1.5 s and 20 ms for all streams; this is the applicable reference for this conversion.\n   142→- Data-paper wheel analysis averages wheel values and spike counts in non-overlapping 20 ms bins. Its causal decoder uses the current plus ten preceding bins and reports R².\n   143→- Data-paper binary stimulus/choice/feedback decoding uses class-weighted L1 logistic regression and balanced accuracy. Neurons from probes in the same session and region are combined because simultaneous probes are not independent.\n   144→- Whisker motion energy is the mean absolute pixel difference between adjacent frames in a whisker-pad bounding box anchored between DLC nose-tip and eye estimates. Left camera is 60 Hz, right is 150 Hz, and body is 30 Hz. The Zhang overview describes the used whisker stream as sampled at 60 Hz, consistent with preferring left camera.\n   145→- Trial task: 90 initial 0.5-prior trials followed by alternating 0.2/0.8 probability-left blocks; choice is made by turning the wheel. Block changes are uncued.\n   146→\n   147→### Curation Steps\n   148→\n   149→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   150→- Data-paper analyses define well-isolated neurons using RIGOR metrics: amplitude >50 μV, noise cutoff <20 μV, and refractory-period-violation criterion.\n   151→- Final paper analyses additionally restrict to Allen CCF gray matter, at least five well-isolated neurons per region/session, and regions recorded in at least two qualifying sessions.\n   152→- In contrast, the Zhang caching code deliberately bins all Kilosort clusters (`qc=None`) and records good-unit labels only as metadata. unexpected values fail validation. |\n   215→| Absolute filtered wheel velocity | `output[...][2,:]` | Native position → 1 kHz linear interpolation → 20 Hz low-pass filtered velocity → absolute value → linear interpolation at trial bin ends → global tertile classes 0/1/2 | `SessionLoader.load_wheel`, `wheel.interpolate_position`, `wheel.velocity_filtered`, `load_target_behavior`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Uses reference wheel-speed definition and temporal grid. |\n   216→| Left/right camera ROI motion energy | `output[...][3,:]` | Prefer left, fallback right; one region per retained cluster. |\n   218→| `bwm_release.csv.subject` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Stable first-appearance unique subject list and per-session index | cohort CSV | Only subjects with retained sessions appear. |\n   219→\n   220→### Key Decisions\n   221→1. **Cohort**: Start from all 459 current-release sessions in `bwm_release.csv`, not the older 354-session snapshot or 21 combined extras. Retain only sessions with all required streams and at least two fully valid trials.\n   222→2. **Neuron curation**: Retain all Kilosort clusters to match the Zhang methods-paper executable pipeline (`qc=None`) and prose (“all neurons”). This yields the paper's 621,733 source clusters before behavior-based session exclusion. Data-paper well-isolated QC statistics are retained as checks but not imposed because that would change the methods-paper decoder dataset.\n   223→3. **Probe handling**: Merge every listed probe from the same EID, because simultaneous probes share behavior and the reference explicitly combines them. Cluster order is CSV probe order, then native cluster row order; spike cluster IDs are remapped accordingly.\n   224→4. **Trial curation**: Apply reaction time 0.08–2.0 s inclusive, required non-NaN events, choice !=0, and feedback-goCue duration ≤10 s. Retain probabilityLeft=0.5 trials. Also require complete finite wheel and selected camera coverage over the whole [-0.5,+1.5] window.\n   225→5. **Temporal grid**: Match executable code: 2 s around `stimOn_times`, 20 ms bins, 100 spike-count bins. Behavior and time input use bin ends -0.48 through +1.50 s. Spike bins are `[start,start+0.02)`, etc.; this deliberate 20 ms offset matches reference behavior interpolation.\n   226→6. **Mixed outputs**: Store every output as `(4,100)`. Per-trial choice and prior are broadcast over time, because a single trial output array cannot mix scalar and time-varying row shapes and the supplied decoder accepts categorical values at every timepoint.\n   227→7. **Discretization**: Use global 1/3 and 2/3 empirical quantiles across all retained finite wheel-speed and whisker values. Apply `np.searchsorted([q1,q2], value, side='right')`, yielding classes 0/1/2. Global thresholds preserve a common physical class meaning across sessions. If tied quantiles collapse (e.g. wheel q1=0), detect this and use equal-frequency rank assignment with deterministic stable tie handling; record the final thresholds/method in metadata and logs.\n   228→8. **Trial number in block**: Compute before filtering, reset whenever `probabilityLeft` changes, and number from 1. This preserves the actual experimental trial position despite excluded trials.\n   229→9. **ALF revisions**: Resolve full current objects explicitly. For tables, choose candidates containing required columns, then greatest row count and latest revision tie-break. For NumPy objects, use matched revision pairs and prefer newest validated pair. Never accept the partial legacy one-column trial object selected by generic SessionLoader in this mixed cache.\n   230→10. **Anatomy**: Use `clusters.channels` to index `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017`, then Beryl mapping. Assert cluster-array lengths equal the selected cluster metrics/spike cluster universe.\n   231→11. **Storage and performance**: Neural trial matrices use uint8 after checking maximum count; inputs float32; outputs uint8; region indices int32. Estimated all-cluster neural payload is ~26.5 GB for 444 preliminary sessions. Process one session at a time, memory-map spike arrays, vectorize histogramming, and avoid loading all spikes globally.\n   232→12. **No data split**: Save complete retained sessions. The supplied decoder performs its own train/validation split; reference caching's 70/10/20 split is not represented in the required format.\n   233→\n   234→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   235→- [ ] Recompute raw cohort totals directly: 459 sessions, 139 subjects, 699 probes, 621,733 clusters, 75,708 label-1 units, 296,090 trials.\n   236→- [ ] For at least three raw trials, compare conversion spike counts to independent `np.histogram2d`/`bincount` calculations using `np.allclose`.\n   237→- [ ] Compare converted time input to `np.arange(-0.48,1.5001,0.02)` with `np.allclose`.\n   238→- [ ] Compare trial-in-block values for trials around index 89/90 and later probability transitions to an independent raw-table calculation with `np.allclose`.\n   239→- [ ] Compare choice/prior conversion for three named raw trials with exact expected mappings and `np.allclose`.\n   240→- [ ] Independently derive wheel velocity from raw wheel position with Brainbox functions and compare interpolated converted values/classes.\n   241→- [ ] Independently interpolate raw camera motion energy for three trials and compare pre-discretization values/classes.\n   242→- [ ] Verify every trial matrix has 100 timepoints, every session has ≥2 trials, all arrays are finite, and category values are contiguous and within declared ranges.\n   243→- [ ] Verify merged neuron counts equal sums of selected cluster rows and every neuron has one valid Beryl index.\n   244→- [ ] Report retained/excluded sessions and trials by reason; delta-F/F is not applicable.\n- Reference trial parameters are `align_time='stimOn_times'`, `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)` seconds, and `binsize=0.02` seconds, producing 100 bins per trial.\n- Spike bins use half-open trial intervals (`times >= start` and `times < end`) and integer spike counts. Returned matrices are neuron by time after transposition.\n- `prepare_data` merges every probe in a session and calls `load_spiking_data` without a QC threshold. Thus all sorted clusters are retained; `clusters.label >= 1` is saved only as `good_clusters` metadata. This differs from pipelines that load only good units and must be checked against the papers/data release.\n- Actual trial mask defaults plus `max_trial_len=10`: reaction time (`firstMovement_times-stimOn_times`) must be 0.08–2.0 s; `feedback_times-goCue_times` must be ≤10 s; required fields (`stimOn_times`, `choice`, `feedback_times`, `probabilityLeft`, `firstMovement_times`, `feedbackType`) must be non-NaN; choice 0 is excluded. Probability-left 0.5 trials are retained (`exclude_unbiased=False`).\n- Wheel speed is `abs(wheel.velocity)`. Whisker motion energy uses left-camera `whiskerMotionEnergy`, falling back to right camera only if left loading is skipped.\n- Behavior samples are sliced with `searchsorted(..., side='left')`. Interpolation points are `linspace(interval_start + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)`, i.e. bin-end times from -0.48 through +1.50 s relative to stimulus onset. Linear interpolation permits extrapolation after checking first/last samples are within one bin of interval boundaries.\n- The methods-paper decoder treats wheel speed and whisker motion energy as continuous regression outputs and choice as categorical. Required 3-class discretization in this task is therefore an intentional downstream-format divergence to be planned later.\n- Reference caching randomly partitions trials 70%/10%/20% after alignment. The requested target format stores complete sessions, so conversion should not pre-partition trials.\n- Potential reference-code issue noted for later consistency review: `align_spike_behavior` combines Python lists with `and`, so its final mask does not robustly combine every behavior validity mask. Conversion should explicitly combine validity across required streams and document the justified robustness difference.\n- Spike/behavior standardization and one-hot encoding occur in the reference model data loader, not during raw caching; converted data should preserve counts/classes and let the supplied decoder perform its own model preprocessing.\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `/app/data/one_cache` is a staged IBL ONE cache. Writable release tables and REST metadata are local; 12 laboratory directories are symlinks into a read-only native dataset mount. Native files are therefore accessed through `/app/data/one_cache/<lab>/Subjects/<subject>/<date>/<number>/...` while remaining immutable.\n- **Issue found and corrected**: the initial `find`/`Path.rglob` inventory did not follow directory symlinks and incorrectly suggested only metadata was present. Inspection of `stage_cache.sh` revealed the intended symlink architecture. Re-running exploration with symlink-following paths exposed all native arrays. Step 2 was reopened and all statistics below were recomputed directly from native files.\n- Release tables are in `2022_Q4_IBL_et_al_BWM/`, `2025_Q3_IBL_et_al_BWM/`, and `Brainwidemap/`. The Zhang repository's `bwm_release.csv` provides the authoritative paper cohort and maps 699 PIDs to 459 EIDs, 139 subjects, labs, dates, session numbers, and probe names.\n- Native organization is `<lab>/Subjects/<subject>/<date>/<number>/alf/`. Trial/wheel/camera files are session-level; spikes and cluster metadata are under `alf/<probe>/pykilosort/`. Revised ALF datasets occur in `#YYYY-MM-DD#` subdirectories.\n- Revision selection for exploration chose, within each paper-cohort session/probe, the candidate full table with the greatest row count and then latest lexical revision as a tie-break. This selected all 459 trial tables and all 699 cluster tables with no missing files. Exact totals match the paper, validating this rule for these objects.\n- Relevant formats: Parquet trial/cluster tables, NumPy arrays for spikes/wheel/camera streams, and CSV cluster UUIDs.\n- Representative EID `02fbb6da-3034-47d6-a61b-7d06c796a830` has 646 trials, 781 clusters, and 24,771,586 spikes on probe00. Native spike times are float64 and spike cluster IDs uint32. The reference preference for left therefore gives the stated 60 Hz stream. Native wheel timestamps and positions are float64; SessionLoader derives velocity/acceleration.\n- Across the 459 paper sessions, left camera time+motion-energy pairs exist for 437 sessions, right pairs for 420, and at least one side for 445. Fourteen sessions lack either required whisker stream and cannot support the specified output.\n\n### Dataset Size (from native data files; paper cohort)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons/clusters (total) | 621,733 across 699 insertions |\n| Well-isolated (`label == 1`) | 75,708 |\n| Clusters / insertion | min 135, median 864, max 2,311; paper mean 889 |\n| Subjects | 139 |\n| Sessions / subject | computed release cohort; 459 sessions total |\n| Sessions | 459 |\n| Insertions | 699 |\n| Trials (raw total) | 296,090 |\n| Trials / session | min 401, median 601, max 1,525 |\n| Trials after Zhang discrete-event mask | 195,781 before continuous-stream coverage filtering |\n| Valid-trial choice distribution | IBL -1: 96,189; +1: 99,592 |\n| Valid-trial probabilityLeft distribution | 0.2: 81,747; 0.5: 27,566; 0.8: 86,468 |\n\n### Available Variables and Native Types\n| Source object | Meaning | Native format/type |\n|---------------|---------|--------------------|\n| `_ibl_trials.table.pqt` | choice, probabilityLeft, event times, contrasts, feedback, reward | Parquet DataFrame; one row/trial |\n| `spikes.times.npy` / `spikes.clusters.npy` | spike timestamps and integer cluster assignments per probe | float64 / unsigned integer NumPy arrays |\n| `clusters.metrics.pqt` and cluster/channel arrays | sorting QC label, depths/channels, UUID/anatomical metadata | Parquet/NumPy |\n| `_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy` / `_ibl_wheel.position.npy` | native wheel trajectory; velocity derived by reference SessionLoader | float64 NumPy arrays |\n| `left/rightCamera.times.npy` and `Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy` | sample times and whisker-region motion-energy traces | matched float64 NumPy arrays |\n\n### Native Sanity Results\n- Summing selected cluster rows gives exactly 621,733, matching the paper. Provided caching code uses 20 ms for all requested streams. |\n| Behavior data time bin | 20 ms after interpolation for reference cache | Zhang caching code; paper dynamic behavior also uses 20 ms bins. |\n| Reward rate | Not stated | No fraction supplied in available methods extract. |\n| Initial unbiased trials | 90 | Task description. |\n| Biased block probabilities | probabilityLeft 0.2 or 0.8; blocks 20–100 trials, empirical mean 51 | Data-paper task description. |\n| Method-paper variables | choice, prior, wheel speed, whisker motion energy | Zhang methods extract. |\n| Method-paper sessions | 433, covering 270 brain regions | Zhang methods extract. |\n\n### Processing Details\n- Method-paper common representation: spike-sorted, temporally binned counts with each trial represented as `N × T`; 2 s at 20 ms gives `T=100`.\n- Choice is aligned to stimulus onset using neural activity from -0.5 to +1.5 s. Prior is stimulus-aligned but its paper-specific window is -0.6 to -0.1 s. Dynamic wheel/whisker analyses in the prose align to first movement and use 0 to +1 s. The supplied caching code instead uses the task-required common stimulus alignment of -0.5 to +1.5 s and 20 ms for all streams; this is the applicable reference for this conversion.\n- Data-paper wheel analysis averages wheel values and spike counts in non-overlapping 20 ms bins. Its causal decoder uses the current plus ten preceding bins and reports R².\n- Data-paper binary stimulus/choice/feedback decoding uses class-weighted L1 logistic regression and balanced accuracy. Neurons from probes in the same session and region are combined because simultaneous probes are not independent.\n- Whisker motion energy is the mean absolute pixel difference between adjacent frames in a whisker-pad bounding box anchored between DLC nose-tip and eye estimates. Left camera is 60 Hz, right is 150 Hz, and body is 30 Hz. The Zhang overview describes the used whisker stream as sampled at 60 Hz, consistent with preferring left camera.\n- Trial task: 90 initial 0.5-prior trials followed by alternating 0.2/0.8 probability-left blocks; choice is made by turning the wheel. Block changes are uncued.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Data-paper analyses define well-isolated neurons using RIGOR metrics: amplitude >50 μV, noise cutoff <20 μV, and refractory-period-violation criterion.\n- Final paper analyses additionally restrict to Allen CCF gray matter, at least five well-isolated neurons per region/session, and regions recorded in at least two qualifying sessions.\n- In contrast, the Zhang caching code deliberately bins all Kilosort clusters (`qc=None`) and records good-unit labels only as metadata. Its prose also says all neurons sorted by Kilosort 2.5. This conversion is for reproducing the Zhang neural-decoding representation, so the all-cluster behavior is a candidate mapping choice to resolve explicitly in Steps 4–5.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Data-paper analyses exclude trials missing choice, probabilityLeft, feedbackType, feedback time, stimOn time, or firstMovement time.\n- Reaction time must be 0.08–2.00 s from stimulus onset to first wheel movement.\n- Zhang code additionally excludes no-choice trials and feedback-goCue durations >10 s, retains initial probabilityLeft=0.5 trials, and requires the same core fields.\n- Release sessions require ≥250 trials, ≥90% correct at 100% contrast in each biased block side, ≥3 incorrect trials after exclusion, and task hardware QC. The Results section's “at least 400 trials” refers to the stricter subset retained for further paper analyses rather than public-release eligibility.\n- Insertions require acceptable whole-recording RIGOR, no major artifacts, recovered histology, and resolved alignment.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy / metric reported in available extract |\n|------------------|-----------------------------------------------|\n| Choice | Balanced accuracy metric described; no numerical value in `methods.txt` |\n| Prior | Included by method paper; no numerical value in available extract |\n| Wheel speed | R² in source papers (continuous regression); no numerical value in available extract |\n| Whisker motion energy | Continuous regression/correlation in method paper; no numerical value in available extract |\n\n### Source-access note\n- Installed environment has no `pdftotext`, Ghostscript, or Python PDF parser. The PDFs are compressed such that `strings` exposes metadata but not article body text. The supplied `/app/methods.txt`, reference code, and release CSV therefore provide the usable reference details. No numerical decoder-accuracy table was available in those sources; this limitation is recorded rather than inventing values.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Papers say | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Release size | Zhang `bwm_release.csv`: 459 EIDs, 699 PIDs | Exact native totals: 459 sessions, 699 probes, 621,733 clusters | Data paper: 459/699/621,733 | Use `bwm_release.csv` as authoritative cohort; exact agreement validates loading/revision selection. |\n| Snapshot tables | Code CSV uses current cohort | 2022 table has 354 sessions; 2025 has exactly all 459; combined has 480 | Current paper release has 459 | Do not use older 354 snapshot or 21 combined extras. |\n| Method-paper session count | Caching loop can process arbitrary selected EIDs | 445 sessions have either camera; 444 have ≥2 jointly covered valid trials | Method paper states 433 sessions | Basic stream presence does not explain 433; likely additional paper video/session QC. task explicitly requires stimulus alignment | Task and executable reference cache take precedence: all variables use stimulus onset and common 100-bin grid. |\n| Behavior timestamps | Reference interpolates at bin ends (-0.48…+1.50 s) | Native camera rates differ (left ~60 Hz, right ~150 Hz) | Whisker metric is camera-frame based | Reproduce reference bin-end linear interpolation, preferring left and falling back right. |\n| Missing behavior | Reference calls `allow_nans=True`; `align_spike_behavior` combines masks incorrectly via Python `and` | 14 no-camera sessions plus one uncovered session; finite coverage leaves 444 sessions | Required output must be defined and sessions need ≥2 trials | Explicitly intersect all trial/stream validity masks and exclude undefined trials/sessions; this fixes a clear implementation bug without changing intended processing. |\n| ALF revisions | Generic SessionLoader can select partial legacy objects in this mixed cache | Full revised tables reproduce exact paper totals | Papers assume curated current objects | Resolve full trial/cluster objects explicitly using cohort paths and validated revision selection; avoid partial legacy objects. |\n| Choice coding | Native IBL values are -1/0/+1 | Valid counts: -1=96,189; +1=99,592 | Task requires left=0, right=1 | Map -1→0 and +1→1; exclude 0. |\n| Prior coding | Native `probabilityLeft` is 0.2/0.5/0.8 | Counts are only those three values | Task prescribes 0/1/2 mapping | Map exactly 0.2→0, 0.5→1, 0.8→2. |\n\n### Final Consistent Understanding\n- The source cohort is the exact 459-session/139-subject/699-insertion current data-paper release in `bwm_release.csv`; native totals reproduce every headline statistic.\n- The applicable methods-paper representation is whole-session, merged-probe spike counts with all Kilosort clusters, Beryl anatomical mapping, 20 ms bins, and a common 2 s trial window.\n- The task-required stimulus alignment overrides variable-specific first-movement windows in prose and agrees with the supplied caching script's executable parameters.\n- Continuous behavior is linearly interpolated to neural bin-end times. Required categorical outputs will be discretized only after interpolation using globally defined training-independent thresholds planned in Step 5.\n- Session inclusion for this task must require all four outputs and at least two jointly valid trials. Native coverage gives at most 444 sessions/136 subjects before any later interpolation or spike/anatomy failures. The paper's 433-session number is recorded as an unresolved additional-QC difference, not forced by arbitrary session removal.\n- Corrected native exploration and coverage scripts are in `/app/cache/`; all subsequent conversion checks will compare directly against their selected raw files.\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| Per-probe `spikes.times`, `spikes.clusters` | `neural[session][trial]` | Merge listed probes, reindex clusters, count spikes in 100 half-open 20 ms bins from stimOn-0.5 to stimOn+1.5; shape `(neurons,100)` | `load_spiking_data`, `merge_probes`, `get_spike_data_per_interval`, `bin_spiking_data` | Store integer counts compactly; validate no count exceeds uint8 before casting. |\n| Relative bin-end times | `input[...][0,:]` | Fixed float32 vector `[-0.48,-0.46,...,1.50]` s | `get_behavior_per_interval` interpolation grid | Continuous, time-varying input. Metadata alignment remains stimulus onset at t=0. |\n| `trials.probabilityLeft` transitions | `input[...][1,:]` | Number trials consecutively elapsed in current block, first trial=1; broadcast over 100 bins | Task-specific derived variable | Count on the original full session before trial filtering so exclusions do not renumber the experimental block. |\n| `trials.choice` | `output[...][0,:]` | Native -1→0 (left), +1→1 (right); broadcast over 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` plus task coding | Native choice 0 is excluded. |\n| `trials.probabilityLeft` | `output[...][1,:]` | 0.2→0, 0.5→1, 0.8→2; broadcast over 100 bins | `bin_behaviors` plus task coding | Exact protocol values only; unexpected values fail validation. |\n| Absolute filtered wheel velocity | `output[...][2,:]` | Native position → 1 kHz linear interpolation → 20 Hz low-pass filtered velocity → absolute value → linear interpolation at trial bin ends → global tertile classes 0/1/2 | `SessionLoader.load_wheel`, `wheel.interpolate_position`, `wheel.velocity_filtered`, `load_target_behavior`, `get_behavior_per_interval` | Uses reference wheel-speed definition and temporal grid. |\n| Left/right camera ROI motion energy | `output[...][3,:]` | Prefer left, fallback right; linear interpolation at trial bin ends; global tertile classes 0/1/2 | `load_target_behavior`, `get_behavior_per_interval`, `bin_behaviors` | Require matched timestamp/value arrays and finite interpolated values. |\n| Cluster channel → `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017` | `brain_region_idx[session]` | Atlas ID→native acronym→Beryl acronym, then global categorical index | `list_brain_regions`, `select_brain_regions` | Preserve `root` if assigned; one region per retained cluster. |\n| `bwm_release.csv.subject` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | Stable first-appearance unique subject list and per-session index | cohort CSV | Only subjects with retained sessions appear. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Cohort**: Start from all 459 current-release sessions in `bwm_release.csv`, not the older 354-session snapshot or 21 combined extras. Retain only sessions with all required streams and at least two fully valid trials.\n2. **Neuron curation**: Retain all Kilosort clusters to match the Zhang methods-paper executable pipeline (`qc=None`) and prose (“all neurons”). This yields the paper's 621,733 source clusters before behavior-based session exclusion. Data-paper well-isolated QC statistics are retained as checks but not imposed because that would change the methods-paper decoder dataset.\n3. **Probe handling**: Merge every listed probe from the same EID, because simultaneous probes share behavior and the reference explicitly combines them. Cluster order is CSV probe order, then native cluster row order; spike cluster IDs are remapped accordingly.\n4. **Trial curation**: Apply reaction time 0.08–2.0 s inclusive, required non-NaN events, choice !=0, and feedback-goCue duration ≤10 s. Retain probabilityLeft=0.5 trials. Also require complete finite wheel and selected camera coverage over the whole [-0.5,+1.5] window.\n5. **Temporal grid**: Match executable code: 2 s around `stimOn_times`, 20 ms bins, 100 spike-count bins. Behavior and time input use bin ends -0.48 through +1.50 s. Spike bins are `[start,start+0.02)`, etc.; this deliberate 20 ms offset matches reference behavior interpolation.\n6. **Mixed outputs**: Store every output as `(4,100)`. Per-trial choice and prior are broadcast over time, because a single trial output array cannot mix scalar and time-varying row shapes and the supplied decoder accepts categorical values at every timepoint.\n7. **Discretization**: Use global 1/3 and 2/3 empirical quantiles across all retained finite wheel-speed and whisker values. Apply `np.searchsorted([q1,q2], value, side='right')`, yielding classes 0/1/2. Global thresholds preserve a common physical class meaning across sessions. If tied quantiles collapse (e.g. wheel q1=0), detect this and use equal-frequency rank assignment with deterministic stable tie handling; record the final thresholds/method in metadata and logs.\n8. **Trial number in block**: Compute before filtering, reset whenever `probabilityLeft` changes, and number from 1. This preserves the actual experimental trial position despite excluded trials.\n9. **ALF revisions**: Resolve full current objects explicitly. For tables, choose candidates containing required columns, then greatest row count and latest revision tie-break. For NumPy objects, use matched revision pairs and prefer newest validated pair. Never accept the partial legacy one-column trial object selected by generic SessionLoader in this mixed cache.\n10. **Anatomy**: Use `clusters.channels` to index `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017`, then Beryl mapping. Assert cluster-array lengths equal the selected cluster metrics/spike cluster universe.\n11. **Storage and performance**: Neural trial matrices use uint8 after checking maximum count; inputs float32; outputs uint8; region indices int32. Estimated all-cluster neural payload is ~26.5 GB for 444 preliminary sessions. Process one session at a time, memory-map spike arrays, vectorize histogramming, and avoid loading all spikes globally.\n12. **No data split**: Save complete retained sessions. The supplied decoder performs its own train/validation split; reference caching's 70/10/20 split is not represented in the required format.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Recompute raw cohort totals directly: 459 sessions, 139 subjects, 699 probes, 621,733 clusters, 75,708 label-1 units, 296,090 trials.\n- [ ] For at least three raw trials, compare conversion spike counts to independent `np.histogram2d`/`bincount` calculations using `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Compare converted time input to `np.arange(-0.48,1.5001,0.02)` with `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Compare trial-in-block values for trials around index 89/90 and later probability transitions to an independent raw-table calculation with `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Compare choice/prior conversion for three named raw trials with exact expected mappings and `np.allclose`.\n- [ ] Independently derive wheel velocity from raw wheel position with Brainbox functions and compare interpolated converted values/classes.\n- [ ] Independently interpolate raw camera motion energy for three trials and compare pre-discretization values/classes.\n- [ ] Verify every trial matrix has 100 timepoints, every session has ≥2 trials, all arrays are finite, and category values are contiguous and within declared ranges.\n- [ ] Verify merged neuron counts equal sums of selected cluster rows and every neuron has one valid Beryl index.\n- [ ] Report retained/excluded sessions and trials by reason; investigate the expected preliminary ceiling of 444 stream-covered sessions and the method-paper value of 433.\n- [ ] Verify output class distributions, especially that all three behavior bins are populated and not pathologically imbalanced.\n- [ ] Estimate conversion time from two-session sample and optimize if projected full runtime exceeds 15 minutes.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n- Created `/app/convert_data.py` with required invocation and mutually exclusive `--full`/`--sample` modes plus `--show-processing`.\n- Implemented explicit full ALF trial-table and matched camera revision resolution, authoritative cohort loading, reference trial mask, left-camera preference/right fallback, exact Brainbox 1 kHz wheel interpolation and filtered velocity, reference bin-end interpolation, global tertiles, merged-probe spike binning, Beryl mapping, metadata, structural assertions, timing logs, and plots.\n- Uses a two-pass design: behavior is aligned and cached per session first; global discretization thresholds are then computed; spike arrays are memory-mapped and binned one session/probe at a time.\n- Neural counts are stored as uint8 when maximum bin count ≤255 and uint16 otherwise. Inputs are float32, outputs uint8, and index arrays int32.\n- Syntax and CLI help checks passed.\n- Smoke test on EID `6713a4a7-faed-4df2-acab-ee4e63326f8d`: 565 raw trials, 407 jointly valid trials, finite wheel/whisker arrays of `(407,100)`. Two spike trials binned successfully for 898 neurons, max count 7, with 7,439 total spikes in the first tested matrix pair.\n- Code-review fixes before sample conversion: replaced endpoint-clamping `np.interp` with reference-matching linear extrapolation via `interp1d`; corrected spike time/cluster mask propagation for unexpected cluster IDs.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Full output is intrinsically large (~26.5 billion count elements before behavior-stage exclusions) because the applicable methods pipeline retains all clusters.\n- Python trial loops remain for variable event windows but expensive spike arrays are memory-mapped and each trial uses binary search plus vectorized `bincount`.\n\nCode speedups added:\n- Two-pass behavior caches avoid recomputing wheel filtering/interpolation during spike processing.\n- Vectorized per-trial spike binning with flattened `np.bincount`.\n- Compact integer output dtypes and pickle protocol 5.\n- Session/probe streaming avoids holding all raw spikes in RAM.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Neurons (summed across sessions) | 2,626 |\n| Neurons / session | 898, 1,728 |\n| Subjects | 1 |\n| Sessions / subject | 2 |\n| Trials (total) | 651 |\n| Trials / session | 407, 244 |\n| Time since stimulus range | [-0.48, 1.50] s (verifier rounds to [-0.5,1.5]) |\n| Trial number in block range | [1, 90] |\n| Choice distribution (per trial) | class 0: 314 (48.2%); This is intentional lossless compact storage of integer spike counts. Full float32 storage would expand the estimated neural payload from ~26.5 GB to ~105.9 GB without adding information; decoder `SessionData` already converts each session to float32. The warning cannot reasonably be eliminated without severe unnecessary storage/I/O cost.\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n|-----------------------|--------------|\n| Compact uint8 counts | ~4× lower pickle size and serialization I/O than float32 |\n| Memory-mapped spikes + vectorized bincount | Avoids loading/copying whole recordings and per-spike Python loops |\n| Behavior cache | Avoids repeated 1 kHz wheel filtering/interpolation |\n\n| Step | Time / Session | Estimated Total Time |\n|------|----------------|----------------------|\n| Behavior alignment | ~0.27 s | ~2.1 min for 459 |\n| Spike binning + plotting in sample | ~0.55 s | ~4.2 min for 459 |\n| Overall sample conversion | 0.85 s/session | ~6.5 min plus large-pickle serialization; expected under 15 min |\n\n---\n\n## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Format Validation\n- Errors: None\n- Warnings: uint8 neural-count storage is converted to float32 by the decoder; justified in Step 7.\n\n### Training Progress\n- Completed all 200 epochs without errors.\n- Loss decreased from the initial high value to 0.601694; test loss was 0.642176.\n- No divergence or late-epoch loss increase was observed.\n\n### Decoder Results (Sample)\n| Output | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Uniform chance |\n|--------|-----------------------|-------------------------|----------------|\n| Choice | 0.7059 | 0.6164 | 0.5000 |\n| Prior probability of left | 0.8320 | 0.7938 | 0.3333 |\n| Wheel speed | 0.6779 | 0.6419 | 0.3333 |\n| Whisker motion energy | 0.7014 | 0.6708 | 0.3333 |\n\nAll outputs exceed chance on held-out trials. The AI loads the full trial table and applies a mask to select valid trials.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef full_trial_table(alf: Path):\n    for p in alf.glob('**/_ibl_trials.table.pqt'):\n        x=pd.read_parquet(p)\n        if REQ_TRIAL.issubset(x.columns): candidates.append((len(x),str(p),p,x))\n```\n\niii. The trials table is already one row per trial. The AI resolves revision ambiguities by preferring the table with the most rows and latest revision.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI applies several filters: (1) required event columns must be non-NaN (stimOn_times, choice, feedback_times, probabilityLeft, firstMovement_times, feedbackType), (2) reaction time (firstMovement_times - stimOn_times) must be 0.08-2.0 s, (3) choice must be non-zero, (4) feedback_times - goCue_times must be <= 10 s. Additionally, trials must have complete finite wheel and camera coverage over the [-0.5, +1.5] window as checked during behavior interpolation.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef trial_mask(x):\n    m=np.ones(len(x),bool)\n    for c in ['stimOn_times','choice','feedback_times','probabilityLeft','firstMovement_times','feedbackType']:\n        m &= x[c].notna().to_numpy()\n    rt=x.firstMovement_times.to_numpy()-x.stimOn_times.to_numpy()\n    m &= (rt >= .08) & (rt <= 2.)\n    m &= x.choice.to_numpy()!=0\n    m &= (x.feedback_times.to_numpy()-x.goCue_times.to_numpy() <= 10.)\n    return m\n```\n\niii. These checks match the Zhang reference code's `load_trials_and_mask` function, which includes the RT bounds, non-NaN field requirements, no-choice exclusion, and the 10-second feedback-goCue duration limit.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. `spikes.times.npy` and `spikes.clusters.npy` per probe, plus `clusters.metrics.pqt` for cluster identification, `clusters.channels.npy` for channel assignments, and `channels.brainLocationIds_ccf_2017.npy` for anatomical locations.\n\nii.\n```python\nst=np.load(p['spikes.times.npy'],mmap_mode='r')\nsc=np.load(p['spikes.clusters.npy'],mmap_mode='r')\n```\n\niii. Spike times and cluster assignments are the fundamental data for constructing neural activity matrices.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spikes are counted into 100 half-open 20 ms bins spanning the trial window [-0.5, +1.5] s around stimulus onset. Counts are stored as raw integer spike counts (uint8 or uint16 depending on maximum count), NOT converted to firing rates. When a session has multiple probes, their clusters are concatenated to form a merged population.\n\nii.\n```python\ntb=np.floor((spike_t-beg)/BIN).astype(int)\nvalid2=(tb>=0)&(tb<NBIN); flat=mapped[valid2]*NBIN+tb[valid2]\na=np.bincount(flat,minlength=n*NBIN).reshape(n,NBIN)\nmats.append(a)\n```\n```python\nif maxcount>255: dtype=np.uint16\nelse: dtype=np.uint8\nneural=[np.concatenate([pmats[p][j] for p in range(len(pmats))],axis=0).astype(dtype,copy=False) for j in range(ntr)]\n```\n\niii. The AI stores raw spike counts rather than firing rates to save space (~26.5 GB vs ~106 GB). The decoder converts to float32 during training. Probe merging follows the reference's `merge_probes` logic.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI retains ALL Kilosort clusters (no quality filtering). It uses `clusters.metrics.pqt` to enumerate cluster IDs but does not filter by the `label` column. All clusters with valid IDs are included, regardless of their sorting quality score.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef resolve_probe(row):\n    ...\n    for p in base.glob('**/clusters.metrics.pqt'):\n        x=pd.read_parquet(p)\n        if 'cluster_id' in x and 'label' in x: candidates.append((len(x),str(p),p,x))\n    ...\n    cluster_ids=metrics.cluster_id.to_numpy(dtype=int)\n    # No label filtering applied\n```\n\niii. The AI justified this by noting that the Zhang reference code's `prepare_data` calls `load_spiking_data` with `qc=None`, retaining all clusters, and the methods paper states \"all neurons sorted by Kilosort 2.5.\" The AI explicitly chose not to apply the data paper's QC label >= 1 filter.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Each trial's spikes are extracted relative to stimulus onset. The window spans from stimOn_times - 0.5 s to stimOn_times + 1.5 s. Binary search (`searchsorted`) identifies the spike indices within this absolute time window, then bins are computed relative to the window start.\n\nii.\n```python\nbeg=s+OFF0; end=s+OFF1\nib=np.searchsorted(st,beg,'left'); ie=np.searchsorted(st,end,'left')\nspike_t=np.asarray(st[ib:ie])\ntb=np.floor((spike_t-beg)/BIN).astype(int)\n```\n\niii. All streams share a common session clock, so alignment is achieved by subtracting the stimulus onset time. This matches the reference approach.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. 20 ms bins, 100 bins per trial, spanning 2 s from -0.5 to +1.5 s around stimulus onset. No rebinning is applied.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN = 0.02\nOFF0, OFF1 = -0.5, 1.5\nNBIN = 100\n```\n\niii. This matches the reference code's `binsize=0.02` and `time_window=(-0.5, 1.5)`.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time since stimulus onset* derived from?\n\ni. This is a synthetic variable derived from the bin timing grid. It represents the time at each bin relative to stimulus onset.\n\nii.\n```python\nREL_TIME = np.linspace(OFF0 + BIN, OFF1, NBIN, dtype=np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The time input is defined by the binning parameters, not from a raw data variable.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time since stimulus onset*?\n\ni. The AI computes bin-end times using `linspace(-0.48, 1.50, 100)`, which gives 100 evenly spaced values from -0.48 to 1.50 s. These are bin ends, not bin centers.\n\nii.\n```python\nREL_TIME = np.linspace(OFF0 + BIN, OFF1, NBIN, dtype=np.float32)\n# = np.linspace(-0.48, 1.50, 100) -> [-0.48, -0.46, ..., 1.50]\n```\n\niii. The AI followed the Zhang reference code's convention of using bin-end times (`linspace(interval_start + binsize, interval_end, n_bins)`), which differs from the human reference that uses bin centers.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time since stimulus onset* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The same `REL_TIME` array is used both as the time input values and as the interpolation grid for behavioral variables. The spike bins cover the same overall window, so the time input is inherently aligned with the neural data (though the time values represent bin ends while spike bins are half-open intervals starting at the bin's left edge).\n\nii.\n```python\ninp=[np.vstack((REL_TIME,np.full(NBIN,tib[j],np.float32))).astype(np.float32) for j in range(ntr)]\n```\n\niii. The behavior and time input share the same 100-element time grid, ensuring alignment.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Trial number in block* derived from?\n\ni. From `probabilityLeft` in the trials table.
